# Cartridges

Nothing in this directory is shared. A cartridge belongs to whoever made it, and no
part of one is carried by this repository, published in the manifest, or
reconstructible from anything here. What the manifest holds is a name, a length and
four digests, and a digest reconstructs nothing.

Put copies you already own here and the tests that need one run against them. Leave
the directory empty and those tests report as skipped rather than as passed, so a run
that proved nothing never reads as a run that proved something.

## Where they go

Here, under any folder arrangement, keeping the filenames below. Subdirectories are
walked, so an existing collection can be pointed at whole rather than copied. Set
`SNES_CARTRIDGE_DIR` to use a library somewhere else; a named directory wins even when
it turns out to be empty, because quietly falling back from a path somebody typed turns
a typo into a run that skips its tests and reports success.

A dump carrying a 512 byte copier stub is accepted and stepped over.

## What is checked

`sha256` decides. The other three are published so a copy can be cross-checked against
a database that keys on them, and all four are confirmed rather than only the deciding
one: a file can be the right length under the right name and still be a bad dump, and a
manifest that publishes a crc32 and never looks at it is publishing decoration.

## What is listed

2778 retail releases across every region. A modified release, a translation and a
prototype can each carry an edited header, and a header read out of one describes
somebody's edit rather than a cartridge that was manufactured, so none are listed.

| Layout | Cartridges |
|--------|-----------:|
| `lorom` | 2,154 |
| `hirom` | 572 |
| `sa1` | 42 |
| `exhirom` | 6 |
| `spc7110` | 4 |

13 of them carry a title of twenty two characters, whose last letter lands on the
mapping byte. For those the place the header sits names the layout, because the byte
that should name it is a letter.

## The law this rests on

A length and a digest are measurements of a file rather than expression, and
measurements sit outside what copyright reaches under 17 U.S.C. 102(b) and Feist
Publications v. Rural Telephone Service. Reading a header to learn how a cartridge is
built is the kind of examination Sega v. Accolade and Sony v. Connectix hold to be fair.
None of that extends to the cartridge itself, which is why it is not here.
